Now — `TestOVSDeviceMissing` calls `t.Parallel()` and both tests in same package; integration test doesn't call Parallel. Fine.

Test naming: `assertReattachKeepsOnePort` — for the "peer delete left the stale port" assertion, message says "want the stale port". Fine.

Now — CI: does any workflow run `go test` where this test would attempt? Let me check quickly: grep "go test" in workflows. If tests run in a container without ovs, skip. Not critical.

Now let me look at the base ovs.go to confirm the previous Delete behavior and AddLinkToContainer (the diff shows it). I've seen enough.

Next, consider `removeBridgePort` naming/behavior in `links/ownership.go`: it references `bridgePortDeleter` interface defined in links package; ovs implements it — creates a hidden coupling from links → nodes/ovs via interface assertion (no import cycle since interface-based). Acceptable Go pattern.

But one design concern: `removeBridgePort` is called within `node.ExecFunction(ctx, ...)` closure — i.e., inside the netns of the node. For the ovs node (root ns based), fine. But `DeleteBridgePort` may be invoked for endpoint of a container-side node? Only ovs implements it. OK.

Another check: `Endpoint.Remove` — the closure captures `e.GetNode()` which for the bridge endpoint is the ovs node; removeBridgePort called there → DeleteBridgePort — uses n.Cfg.ShortName as bridge. Good.

Now potential nil deref: `n.Config().ShortName` in Delete's log — `Config()` returns Cfg; if Cfg nil? Delete is called on a deployed node; fine. Note removeEndpointPorts guards n.Cfg == nil.

Now — think about `Delete` on an ovs node whose bridge pre-existed with unrelated ports (per docs, containerlab does NOT create OVS bridges — users create them!). Important: docs say "Containerlab doesn't create bridges on users behalf" — the OVS bridge is user-created and persists after `clab destroy` (Delete only removes ports). So `removeMissingAutoPorts` operates on a user-owned, possibly long-lived bridge shared across MANY labs. Consider: user runs lab A on bridge `br-ovs`, then lab B (same bridge name via same node shortname? node shortname is per-lab node name...). Two different labs can each have an ovs-bridge node with ShortName = the SAME bridge (e.g., both "ovsbr0"). Lab B's `Delete` will call removeMissingAutoPorts on the shared bridge and delete any missing clab-* ports — including ports that belong to lab A endpoints that are currently stopped (netdev deleted when the lab A container is stopped? If lab A is merely destroyed... then its ports are stale anyway and lab A's own Delete cleans them). What about lab A still RUNNING: its ports' netdevs exist → not deleted. OK. And what about a lab A endpoint whose container was restarted (docker restart recreates the netns but veths are recreated by docker; the clab veth gets deleted on container stop → port becomes missing → lab B's destroy would clean lab A's stale port... but lab A's own redeploy re-creates it. Hmm — actually if lab A's container restarts, its veths die and OVS ports go stale; lab B destroy cleaning them is *helpful*.

Edge: ovsMapValue and `find Interface external_ids:containerlab-endpoint="..."` — the tag is set on the Interface row. When two labs share a bridge and both have an endpoint with the same iface name... iface names in root ns are globally unique anyway.

OK. Now the more serious potential correctness issue: **`removeMissingAutoPorts` may delete a live port's... no.** Only missing ones.

But WAIT — there's one real hazard: the check is `ovs-vsctl get Interface <port> error` — the `error` column reflects the last datapath open attempt. For a port whose netdev EXISTS but ovs-vswitchd hasn't yet refreshed (e.g., just attached and error column stale from previous missing state)? Sequence: port existed with missing netdev (error set). Then a NEW netdev with the same name appears (e.g., the endpoint is reattached with the same random name? randName is per endpoint instance; a new deploy creates a new endpoint → new random name. But redeploy of same containerlab process? Endpoint re-created → new randName. However, the SAME random name could recur across deploys? genRandomString uses uuid v4 → 8 hex chars from a random UUID — collision probability across deploys negligible (16^8 = 4B). But actually a more realistic path: netdev with the same name reappears — e.g., the user re-created the veth manually, or docker restart recreates container netns with the same interface name... For clab-* random names, the name comes from the endpoint instance, so reattach creates a new netdev with a NEW name, and the OLD port keeps the old name with stale error. Then: attach flow — DeleteBridgePort(logical): find by tag finds OLD port (tagged with logical) → deletes it. Good, before AddPort. So no stale-error race.

But consider TWO endpoints with 16+ char names attached to the same bridge: endpoint1 port clab-A (live, error=[]), endpoint2 attach: removeMissingAutoPorts: gets error of clab-A → "[]" → skip. Good.

Now, what about the OVS `error` column semantics for internal ports or just-added ports — the error column may be set for live interfaces in some cases (e.g., "could not open network device" happens when ovs-vswitchd can't find the netdev). If OVS hasn't revalidated yet after netdev creation... scenario: AddPort(clab-B) → ovs-vswitchd adds the interface; between AddPort and ovs-vswitchd actually opening the netdev, the error column might briefly show an error? For a newly added port, ovs-vswitchd opens the netdev when it processes the transaction — the error column is set at that point. If we query `get Interface clab-B error` right after AddPort in a LATER attach... error would be [] by then. Within the same attach, we don't query. Between AddPort (attach k) and next attach (k+1), ovs-vswitchd will have processed. But there's a race: ovs-vsctl returns when the DB transaction commits; ovs-vswitchd picks it up asynchronously. So at attach k+1, port clab-B's error column might still be stale/empty — empty is fine (skip). The dangerous direction: error stale from previous missing state while netdev now exists → would be deleted! Scenario: port clab-B existed in a previous deploy with missing netdev (error set). New deploy attaches a NEW endpoint (new random name) — not the same port name. So no.

What if the same random name recurs (4B chance)? Skip.

What about the endpoint with a VALID name: port "eth1" with error set (netdev gone). Reattach: LinkByName notfound → in Endpoint.Remove... but at deploy, CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet? EndpointBridge.Verify → CheckEndpointDoesNotExistYet — ensures iface doesn't exist. But the OVS port named "eth1" might exist from previous deploy with error set. Deploy: AddLinkToContainer → DeleteBridgePort("eth1") → removeEndpointPorts → DeletePort removes the stale port. Good.

Alright, now the *other* main risk: `removeMissingAutoPorts` uses regex `^clab-[0-9a-f]{8}$` — what about `clab-` names of 8 hex chars that are live netdevs of endpoints with VALID iface names? No—those are renamed.

Hmm wait, actually there's an important case: endpoint with valid iface name — SetNameMACAndUpInterface renames the netdev from clab-XXXXXXXX to the iface name. But `AddPort` is called with `link.Attrs().Name` AFTER re-read → the final name. So port name = iface name. The random name only remains for invalid ifaces. Consistent with the comment.

Now — what about the case where the netdev keeps a random name AND the name matches `clab-[0-9a-f]{8}`? That's exactly the auto-port. But ALSO: what if an endpoint's iface name is invalid (16 chars) and there's an altname; OVS port named clab-B. Its netdev exists → keep. Fine.

Now consider `Delete` for an endpoint with a valid iface name that was NEVER attached (deploy failed earlier): DeletePort --if-exists no-op. Fine.

Now, is there an issue with `ovsInterfaceNames` returning Interface names but `bridgePortSet` containing PORT names — for OVS, a port can have multiple interfaces (bonds). For clab veths, 1:1. The find returns Interface row names; port names equal interface names for single-interface ports. OK.

Next: check for tests in links package affected by the changed `Remove`/`RemoveOwnedInterface` behavior. Existing tests: links tests calling Remove with fake nodes — fake nodes don't implement bridgePortDeleter → no change. Check `removeBridgePort(node...)` with node == nil: Endpoint.Remove's closure: e.GetNode() could be nil for nodeless endpoints? EndpointGeneric.Remove is called for all endpoints during destroy; for mgmt bridge endpoints, node is mgmtBridgeLinkNode (non-nil). In the test file, `NewEndpointGeneric(nil, "x", nil)` — nil node, but test doesn't call Remove. Any real path where Remove is called with nil node? moveEndpoint guards. Destroy flows have nodes. The nil guard in removeBridgePort handles it anyway.

Now check `clab destroy` flow: for each node, node.Delete → ovs.Delete. And links removal: links.Remove → ep.Remove. Fine.

Now — documentation check: the PR changes the destroy behavior (OVS ports cleaned even when netdev gone). Should docs mention? Not necessarily. Release note: this is a bug fix; the repo's rn files track releases. Optional. I'll note in coverage.

Now let me check `docs/rn` for the current unreleased note? The instruction from release-note-review rule: inspect changes under docs/rn — none changed. OK.

Now let me also check the git-mutations rule (not relevant).

One more thing to verify — `ovs.go` head vs the diff: the diff shows the full changes. Also check whether `n.logicalEndpointName` handles `GetEndpoints()` returning endpoints whose iface name differs in case or with aliases. Fine.

Potential issue: **`logicalEndpointName` returns the iface name whose SANITIZED name is in altnames — but the altname may have been added by a PREVIOUS attach of a different endpoint?** Altnames on a netdev: SetNameMACAndUpInterface adds (1) sanitized iface (if invalid), (2) sanitized alias (if any), (3) ownership altname. The netdev is fresh per attach (created by veth deploy with random name). So altnames belong to this endpoint only. But wait — in `moveLink` (endpoint.go:325), an existing link is moved to dst node and `dst.AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link, noop)` — the link already has altnames from its previous owner (ownership altname for src node!). Then logicalEndpointName: alts contains the OLD ownership hash altname and the old sanitized iface altname (iface name unchanged by move — the link keeps its iface name). The endpoints of dst node: the moved endpoint (adopted). `iface == name`? The link's name IS the iface name (it kept its name in moveLink... actually moveLink: LinkByName(ifaceName) in src ns, then AddLinkToContainer with noop f — name unchanged). So iface == name matches → logical = iface. Fine.

But what about a link moved to an OVS node where the iface name is invalid? Can a link move to ovs happen with an invalid name? moveLink is used for parking/moving endpoints between nodes. If the moved endpoint attaches to ovs node... moveLink → dst.AddLinkToContainer (ovs) → f is noop → link keeps random clab-* name and OLD altnames (ownership of src). logicalEndpointName: endpoint iface = X (the original topology iface name); alts contain SanitizeInterfaceName(X) from the original attach (if X invalid) — wait, when the endpoint was first attached to src node, SetNameMACAndUpInterface added altname SanitizeInterfaceName(X). On move, altnames persist. So alts[SanitizeInterfaceName(X)] → matches → logical = X. AddPort(clab-*) + tag X. Correct!

Good, the altname mechanism carries across moves.

Now — one more possible bug: In AddLinkToContainer, `DeleteBridgePort(logical)` when logical is the netdev's own random name (no endpoint match): DeletePort(bridge, clab-B) — the port doesn't exist yet, --if-exists fine. OK.

Now — think about `ovsMapValue("")` returns `""` (quoted). Used in find condition `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=""` — matches interfaces tagged with empty string? Never set to empty. removeEndpointPorts guards endpoint == "". OK.

Now — the severity assessment of the quadratic ovs-vsctl exec issue. Per attach:
- removeEndpointPorts: 1 DeletePort, 1 find, 1 ListPorts → 3 execs
- removeMissingAutoPorts: 1 ListPorts + M gets (M = number of clab-* ports on bridge) → execs

Note removeEndpointPorts and removeMissingAutoPorts BOTH call ListPorts — 2 ListPorts per DeleteBridgePort.

For a topology with N long-named endpoints on one OVS bridge: attach k has k-1 clab ports live → total execs ≈ Σ_{k=1..N} (4 + (k-1) + ...) ≈ N²/2 + 5N. N=100 → ~5500 execs. Each ovs-vsctl call: process spawn + unix socket RPC ≈ 10-50ms → 1-5 minutes added for large OVS topologies. And Delete path similar. This is worth a P3 (performance regression in the deploy hot path; correctness fine).

Actually wait — is it really per-attach? AddLinkToContainer calls DeleteBridgePort once per attach. Yes. And the Delete path calls it once per endpoint + once more.

Hmm, but one subtlety: for endpoints with VALID names (the common case for OVS bridges? e.g., "ovsp1"), the port is named after the iface, NOT clab-*. So M (number of clab-* ports) = number of invalid-named endpoints only. The scan still lists all ports and only runs `get` per clab-* port. So the quadratic cost applies when many endpoints have invalid (16+ char) names — which is realistic for realistic topology names like "ixr-dut-30-e1-10" (16 chars) — the PR's own test uses exactly this. And with valid names, the per-attach overhead is still ~4-5 execs vs 1 before (3x constant factor). The N² only when many long names. I'll frame it accurately.

Correction suggestion: in `AddLinkToContainer`, only call `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` (the targeted path) instead of `DeleteBridgePort`, since the tag covers stale random-named ports from this version; keep the missing-scan in `Delete` only (already runs there) — or pass the known stale port. Alternatively reuse one port listing. Simplest: change `DeleteBridgePort` to not run the missing-scan, and call `removeMissingAutoPorts` explicitly where needed (Delete, and DeleteBridgePort keeps it for the external callers via removeBridgePort). Hmm — external callers (removeBridgePort from Endpoint.Remove / RemoveOwnedInterface) rely on the missing-scan? For the notfound case: the port named after the iface may exist with a missing netdev (valid-name case) → removeEndpointPorts' DeletePort covers it directly (--if-exists). For the invalid-name case: tagged port → find covers it. Untagged (pre-upgrade) random-name port → only the missing-scan covers it. So the scan matters for upgrade leftovers. In `Delete` it runs anyway. So `AddLinkToContainer` could call `removeEndpointPorts` only. And Delete's per-endpoint loop calling DeleteBridgePort (which scans each time) is redundant given the final scan — could call removeEndpointPorts directly + final scan. That reduces execs from ~5+M per endpoint to 3 per endpoint + 1 scan.

Now, let me also double check `RemoveOwnedInterface`'s new notfound path behavior in the reconcile flow: `deleteApplyEndpoints` — for ovs endpoints, notfound now triggers removeBridgePort → DeleteBridgePort → good (fixes stale port on apply/destroy of individual endpoints).

Also check: does `RemoveOwnedInterface` get called for ovs-side endpoints in `apply.go:321`? Let me peek at apply.go around 300-330 for context.

Reasoning
Confirmed the base state. Now let me double-check a few remaining things:

1. The `link.go` line where `Endpoint` iface names with '/' or ' ' — SanitizeInterfaceName replaces those. But `IsValidInterfaceName` only rejects space and slash. What about a name containing ':' or '@' (allowed by ovsMapValue safe set)? Kernel allows most chars except '/' and NUL. OK.

2. Check whether `netlink.LinkAddAltName` altnames are limited to 15 chars (ALTIFNAMSIZ = 128 in Linux 5.10+). Fine.

3. One more important check: `logicalEndpointName` — if the topology iface name is valid, but the containerlab-generated random name ALSO ends up as an altname? No.

But hold on: for the CONTAINER-side endpoint of a link to ovs: e.GetNode() is the container node; removeBridgePort no-ops since container node doesn't implement DeleteBridgePort. But the OVS port leak scenario: container-side endpoint Remove runs LinkDel of the container-side veth → the whole veth pair dies → the ovs-side endpoint netdev disappears. Then the ovs-side endpoint Remove runs (during destroy, endpoints iterate) → notfound → removeBridgePort(ovs node) → cleans port. But what if the ovs-side endpoint's Remove is NOT called (e.g., destroy removes nodes; node.Delete → ovs.Delete iterates its endpoints → DeleteBridgePort). Both paths covered.

7. One more potential issue: In `Endpoint.Remove`, when LinkByName fails with a non-notfound error (e.g., netlink error), returns err — unchanged.

8. `removeBridgePort` in `RemoveOwnedInterface`: after `LinkDel(link)` succeeded, `removeBridgePort(node, ifaceName)` — for ovs, DeleteBridgePort(iface) → removeEndpointPorts → DeletePort (port might still exist with error after netdev deleted — OVS removes the port when? When ovs-vswitchd notices the netdev is gone, the Interface row gets the error column; the Port row persists). So DeletePort removes it. Good.

Hmm — actually wait. When a netdev that is an OVS port is deleted, does OVS automatically remove the port from the bridge? I recall that ovs-vswitchd does NOT auto-remove ports when the netdev disappears; the port remains in the DB with an error on the interface. Yes — that's the leak this PR fixes. Good.

9. Now, verify there's no issue with calling `DeleteBridgePort` from `removeBridgePort` while inside `ExecFunction` netns — `ovs-vsctl` connect to unix socket — fine.

10. Check for a subtle bug in `removeEndpointPorts`: after DeletePort(endpoint), it does `find Interface external_ids:...=endpoint` and deletes those ports on THIS bridge only. But what if the tag exists on an Interface whose Port is on this bridge but the port name is NOT in bridgePortSet because it's a "fake bridge"/vlan? Edge; skip.

11. `bridgePortSet` — ListPorts returns port names. OK.

15. One more consideration — `ovsMapValue` produces Go-quoted strings for values with special chars. Topology iface names can contain e.g. `*` or `$`? If the iface name is invalid (space/slash/len>15), sanitized name replaces only space and slash — other chars like `(`, `)`, `#` pass through as altname and would be ovsMapValue-quoted with Go %q — Go %q on "a(b)" produces `"a(b)"` — fine for ovs-vsctl (escaped chars: `"` and `\` handled similarly; Go escapes non-ASCII with \u which ovs may not accept). Extremely edge; skip.

**Finding 6 (P3?)**: The missing ownership gate in the notfound path of `RemoveOwnedInterface`/`Remove`: now, ANY destroy of an ovs-node endpoint with a missing netdev deletes the OVS port named/tagged with that iface without verifying the interface was containerlab-owned. When lab1 is destroyed, its "eth1" netdev dies; if lab2 is destroyed after, lab2's endpoint "eth1" (if same name — but they'd have collided at deploy). So can't coexist. Low risk. Skip or P4. I'll skip it — no concrete trigger beyond contrived cases, and the sweep is deliberate.

**Finding 7 (P4, style/consistency)**: `logicalEndpointName` builds an `alts` map — could use `slices.Contains` on AltNames (already imported `slices` in endpoint.go but not ovs.go). Superficial; skip. Karpathy: don't nitpick.

**Finding 8**: Documentation — no user-facing doc updates for the changed destroy/cleanup behavior. The OVS kind doc doesn't document port cleanup behavior at all (before or after). Not a regression in docs. Skip.

**Finding 9**: `setOVSEndpointID` runs `ovs-vsctl` via `exec.Command` while the rest of the file uses the goOvs library — inconsistency; the library can't set external_ids on Interface? go-openvswitch does support `ovs.VSwitch.Set.Interface`? It has `c.ovs.exec("set", "Interface", ...)`. Actually goOvs has `Client.VSwitch.Set.Interface(name, ...)`? There's `VSwitch.Set` API... The library's ovs package exposes `Set.Interface`? I recall `ovs.VSwitch.Set.Interface(name, fields...)`. If it does, using it would avoid direct exec and keep the command path consistent (goOvs also shells out to ovs-vsctl). Minor consistency point; P4. Let me check the library API availability — can't (module not in snapshot). I'll frame it as "prefer the existing go-openvswitch client if it supports setting external_ids" — risky claim. Hmm, the library does have `func (c *VSwitch) Set...`? I'm not certain. I'll skip this or mention as P4 with hedging. Actually the exec.Command approach is simple and works; ponytail says boring. Skip.

Let me also double check `strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")` — with `--data=bare`, the error set is printed with quotes around each string: `"could not open network device clab-x (No such device)"`. The Contains handles quotes. But what about `--format` default (table)? Default format for `get` is... `ovs-vsctl get` output for a set with --data=bare: bare values, unquoted? The docs: `--data=bare` → never quotes data. So output: `could not open network device clab-x (No such device)` unquoted. And empty set prints `[]`. Both match Contains. The test uses the quoted variant — also matched. Fine.

Wait — with `--data=bare`, is the empty set printed as `[]`? I believe bare output of an empty set is `[]`. Yes.

Now — one more important check: `ovsInterfaceNames` uses `--format=csv --no-headings --data=bare` — with CSV format, each name on its own line, bare. Good.

Another thing: `find` on `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint=<value>` — ovs-vsctl find conditions on map columns: syntax `external_ids:containerlab-endpoint="value"` works. For bare unquoted values, ovs-vsctl accepts unquoted strings for map values? `ovs-vsctl find Interface external_ids:foo=bar` — I believe unquoted bare values work in conditions. Yes.

Now, the FINAL check — **the `error` column check could false-positive on other errors**: any error set containing "could not open network device" — also occurs for interfaces with type mismatch etc. It's a heuristic with a comment; the regex gate restricts to clab-* names. Acceptable; ponytail-ceiling comment? The comment explains the approach. Fine.

One more potential issue: **`removeMissingAutoPorts` runs even when called via `removeBridgePort` for a *non-bridge* context?** Only ovs implements it. Fine.

And: `DeleteBridgePort` is exported on the ovs type — naming consistent.

Now, let me reconsider the **P2 candidate**: In `AddLinkToContainer`, `DeleteBridgePort(logical)` is called BEFORE `AddPort`. But `removeEndpointPorts(logical)` does `DeletePort(bridge, endpoint)` FIRST — for the valid-name case, this deletes an existing live port (from a previous deploy where the netdev existed and was LIVE — e.g., partial redeploy where the old container still runs? Deploy flow ensures the old container is gone). Fine.

And in the **redeploy without container recreation** (e.g., `clab deploy` on an already-deployed lab — no-op links skip? DeployLinks only deploys links being created). Fine.

Now let me also verify there's no issue with the test's use of `n.Delete(ctx)` — `ovs.Delete` calls `n.Config().ShortName` in error paths only. Fine.

Now — check the `links` package tests that might exercise `Remove` with a fake node that now... `removeBridgePort` type-asserts; fake nodes unaffected.

Let me also quickly scan `links/apply_test.go` for ownership tests around RemoveOwnedInterface — line 84-231 shown earlier — those test isOwnedBy etc. The changed paths add removeBridgePort calls — for fake nodes, no-op. Tests should pass.

Now, severity of finding 1 (perf). Let me be careful about the exec counts:

DeleteBridgePort(endpoint):
- removeEndpointPorts: goOvs DeletePort (1 exec), ovsInterfaceNames find (1 exec), bridgePortSet ListPorts (1 exec) = 3 execs
- removeMissingAutoPorts: ListPorts (1 exec) + per clab-* port: ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing (1 exec each) + DeletePort per missing.

So per attach: ≥4 execs + O(P) gets. Before: 1 exec. For a topology with N endpoints with >15-char names on one ovs bridge (the exact case this PR targets, e.g. "ixr-dut-30-e1-10"): P grows to N → total ≈ Σ(4+k) ≈ N²/2. N=100 → ~5400 execs. Each ovs-vsctl: fork + ovsdb RPC ~5–30 ms → 30s–2.5min. Also in `Delete` per endpoint. This is inference from code (no measurements) — state that. P3.

Actually — wait. Let me re-check: does `removeMissingAutoPorts` run `get` for EVERY clab-* port, including live ones? Yes — it queries error for each matching port and skips only if not missing. So each attach queries all live clab-* ports. Yes, O(P) per attach.

Could we suggest the smallest fix? In `AddLinkToContainer`, replace `n.DeleteBridgePort(logical)` with `n.removeEndpointPorts(logical)`. And in `Delete`, call `n.removeEndpointPorts(ep.GetIfaceName())` in the loop and keep the single trailing `removeMissingAutoPorts()` (already present). That's a small diff, removes the redundant sweeps. But careful: `DeleteBridgePort` is also the external hook via `removeBridgePort` — keep it as is for those callers (they need the sweep for pre-upgrade leftovers on endpoint removal too — actually for removeBridgePort callers, removeEndpointPorts covers tagged+named; the sweep covers pre-upgrade leftovers — keep).

Hmm — but wait, in the notfound path of `RemoveOwnedInterface`, removeBridgePort → DeleteBridgePort → sweep — for a valid-name port with missing netdev, removeEndpointPorts' DeletePort covers it. Fine.

So the suggestion: avoid the full sweep in the per-attach and per-endpoint-Delete loops; keep it for node-level cleanup and external endpoint removal. Alternatively reuse one port listing per call. I'll present as P3.

Second thought on Finding 2 severity: also within `Delete`'s loop, `removeMissingAutoPorts` errors abort the loop? No — Delete logs the error and continues to the next endpoint. In AddLinkToContainer it aborts deploy. OK.

Now, another candidate finding — **`ovs.go` line 166-171: `DeleteBridgePort(logical)` when `logical == link.Attrs().Name` (valid name case)**: removeEndpointPorts does 3 execs (DeletePort no-op, find returns empty, ListPorts). Wasteful but small. Included in finding 1.

Now let me double check the claim "go-openvswitch DeletePort uses --if-exists" — the base code comment (authoritative, written in base) states it. Keep.

Now, is there anything about `ovs_test.go` needing a runnable check per ponytail? It has tests. Good.

One more check — `ovs.go` imports: `os/exec`, `regexp`, `strings`, `clabutils` — all used. `fmt` used (Errorf). OK.

Now, let me check the `links/ownership.go` placement: `bridgePortDeleter` interface + `removeBridgePort` defined BETWEEN RemoveOwnedInterface and isOwnedBy — fine.

Also confirm `removeBridgePort` handles `node == nil` — yes.

Edge: `ifaceName == ""` guard — from RemoveOwnedInterface already guarded; from Endpoint.Remove, e.GetIfaceName() could be ""? Endpoints have names. Fine.

Now, let me quickly verify the head ovs.go `Delete` uses `n.DeleteBridgePort(ep.GetIfaceName())` — yes line 89.

One more possible issue — **`AddLinkToContainer` calls `DeleteBridgePort` which calls `removeMissingAutoPorts`, which could delete the port of the endpoint being attached if the netdev... no, not added yet.**

And **`DeleteBridgePort` before AddPort for the 16-char case**: removeEndpointPorts does find by tag — but the CURRENT attach hasn't tagged yet; the stale tag from previous attach exists → deleted. Good.

Now — a genuinely interesting one: **In `AddLinkToContainer`, the pre-add `DeleteBridgePort(logical)` runs `removeEndpointPorts`, whose `DeletePort(bridge, endpoint)` deletes a port named exactly `endpoint` even if it belongs to a LIVE endpoint of the same name on ANOTHER ovs node sharing the bridge**... same-name endpoint on two ovs nodes sharing a bridge: root-ns iface collision prevents simultaneous existence. Skip.

Now finalize findings:

1. P3 — O(N²) ovs-vsctl sweep per attach/endpoint-delete in `DeleteBridgePort` (nodes/ovs/ovs.go:115-121, 251-284) — perf regression vs single AddPort call; suggest scoping the sweep.

2. P3 — `removeMissingAutoPorts` error propagation aborts attach on concurrently-removed port rows (ovs.go:266-269 / 338-352) — suggest tolerating "no row" errors per port (treat as missing) instead of failing the deploy.

Hmm, actually for #2, a cleaner correction: treat ovs-vsctl failure to find the Interface row as "device missing" (the row is gone ⇒ the port is gone anyway) — i.e., handle the not-found error specially. Or check row existence. Suggest: on `get Interface <port> error` failure indicating no such row, treat as removable rather than erroring.

3. P4 — test robustness: `TestOVSEndpointPortDoesNotAccumulate` fails (not skips) when ovs-vsctl is present but ovsdb isn't running; also mutates a globally-named bridge. Suggest skipping on AddBridge failure or probing ovsdb connectivity.

4. Coverage notes: no schema/doc impact; no docs/rn changes; concurrent deploy+destroy races noted; exec-count estimates are inference.

Actually, let me reconsider #3 — is it worth a finding? The ponytail rule: "non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind" — the PR does leave tests. The skip-vs-fail behavior: `exec.LookPath("ovs-vsctl")` succeeds when the binary is installed but the service is down → AddBridge fails → test failure. On GitHub runners ovs isn't installed → skip. For local devs with ovs installed (common for containerlab users testing ovs topologies!) but service not running (they may use a dockerized ovs), the unit suite would fail. That's a plausible annoyance → P4. I'll include it.

Also let me double-check `os.Geteuid() != 0` — on non-Linux (macOS dev), Geteuid works; the test would skip on root check only if not root... on macOS netlink.LinkAdd fails anyway → but root on macOS... The test would fail on macOS when run as root. Edge; skip.

Now, one more sweep of the changed lines for anything I missed. In `Endpoint.Remove`:

```go
case notfound:
    return removeBridgePort(e.GetNode(), e.GetIfaceName())
```

Hmm — one consideration: this runs for EVERY endpoint remove of every link type; for non-ovs nodes it's a type assertion — cheap. Fine.

But wait — there's a subtle behavior change worth flagging? In `RemoveOwnedInterface`, the notfound path now can return an error from `DeleteBridgePort` (ovs-vsctl failures) — e.g., during `clab destroy` when ovs-vswitchd is down, destroy of an ovs lab now FAILS at endpoint removal, whereas previously it succeeded (base: notfound → nil; and for found path, LinkDel failure propagated too). Hmm: in base, if ovs is down: endpoint found (netdev exists) → LinkDel succeeds → port stays (the old leak). Now: LinkDel succeeds → removeBridgePort → ovs-vsctl fails → destroy fails with error. Is that a regression? The destroy now surfaces the failure — arguably correct (fail loudly), but it changes destroy robustness: `clab destroy` previously completed despite ovs issues. Hmm, actually in the found path of `RemoveOwnedInterface`, base returned LinkDel result. New: LinkDel then sweep; sweep failure → destroy fails. For `Endpoint.Remove`, same. So a transient ovs-vsctl failure now aborts destroy mid-way. Destroy flows elsewhere are usually best-effort... Let me check how destroy handles endpoint removal errors — in destroy, link removal errors probably propagate and abort destroy of remaining links. If ovs is unavailable, previously destroy would still proceed (the leak was silent); now it errors out and remaining cleanup stops. Tradeoff. Is this a defect? The error handling is arguably correct (you can't clean the port, tell the user). But destroy aborting means other cleanup doesn't happen. Hmm. I'd rate this a P4/P3 note about best-effort semantics. Let me check how `Remove` errors are handled in the destroy path — grep for where links are removed in destroy.

### Active
- Drafting findings; leading candidate findings:
  1. **P3 performance**: `DeleteBridgePort` now runs per attach in `AddLinkToContainer` (ovs.go) including `removeMissingAutoPorts`, which does 1 ListPorts + one `ovs-vsctl get error` exec per clab-* port → ~O(N²) ovs-vsctl process invocations for N long-named endpoints on one bridge (each exec = fork + ovsdb RPC, est. tens of ms; ~5+P execs per attach vs 1 before). The tag-based `removeEndpointPorts` find already covers stale ports created by this version; the missing-scan at attach time mainly handles pre-upgrade untagged leftovers. Suggested simplification: call `removeEndpointPorts` in AddLinkToContainer, keep the missing-scan in `Delete` (already runs once after the endpoint loop); also avoid duplicate ListPorts (`removeEndpointPorts` and `removeMissingAutoPorts` both call ListPorts) and redundant per-endpoint scans inside `Delete`'s loop.
  2. Possible coverage note (not finding): `removeMissingAutoPorts`' `get Interface <port> error` can error if the row disappears between ListPorts and get (concurrent destroy of a shared bridge) → would fail the link deploy; sequential deploys make this rare.

Test robustness: skip only checks root + ovs-vsctl LookPath, fails if ovs-vsctl installed but ovsdb service not running (P4 at most). Test message has a trailing missing "want 0" (trivial).

So `DefaultNode.Delete` returns the first error from `Link.Remove`. During `clab destroy`, if an OVS cleanup (`removeBridgePort`) fails (e.g., ovs-vswitchd down or transient ovs-vsctl error), the destroy now fails for that link and `DefaultNode.Delete` aborts — leaving the container not deleted. But wait — in destroy, links are removed BEFORE nodes (via `destroyLinks`?)... Let me check how destroy orchestrates: in destroy.go, is there a link-removal loop? The grep didn't show it. Links are removed through node.Delete endpoints (DefaultNode.Delete) or through `cc.destroy` → runtime deletes containers, then nodes deleted. Let me check where links get removed in destroy — probably `c.destroy()` → for each node, node.Delete. Actually links removal during destroy happens via `DefaultNode.Delete` (each node removes its endpoints' links) and `GenericLinkNode.Delete`. Both abort on first error. Base behavior: base `Remove` returned errors too (from LinkByName err or LinkDel err). New: additional error sources from ovs-vsctl cleanup. If ovs is unavailable, before: destroy succeeded with silent leaks; after: destroy fails mid-way. Hmm — but is the ovs node itself the one calling removeBridgePort? During destroy of a lab with an ovs bridge node: `DefaultNode.Delete` for the OVS NODE? The ovs kind has no container; its Delete iterates ITS endpoints. The container nodes' Delete removes veth links — container-side endpoint Remove runs LinkDel (veth pair dies) — then for links to an ovs bridge, the ovs-side endpoint is an endpoint of the OVS node, not the container. `e.GetLink().Remove(ctx)` for a veth link from container's perspective — link_veth.go:334 `ep.Remove(ctx)` for both endpoints. So the ovs-side endpoint Remove runs during the CONTAINER node's Delete (the link belongs to both endpoints). So the notfound/found paths in Endpoint.Remove run in the container node's Delete context, and an ovs-vsctl failure there aborts that container node's Delete → destroy of that container aborted → container not deleted. Base: no ovs-vsctl call → no new failure mode.

So the new error propagation through `Endpoint.Remove` → `DefaultNode.Delete` → aborts container deletion on ovs-vsctl failure. Previously, ovs unavailability didn't block destroy. This is a real behavior regression worth flagging: P3 (reliability). The fix: make the OVS cleanup best-effort (log, don't fail) in the destroy/removal path — e.g., have `removeBridgePort` log errors instead of returning them, or tolerate errors in remove path. But the apply/reconcile path (deleteApplyEndpoints) wants errors. Hmm. Simplest correction: in `removeBridgePort`, log the error and return nil (cleanup is best-effort; leaks are better than failed destroys). But then apply paths lose error signal — they only log too? `deleteApplyEndpoints` bestEffort. I'd suggest: keep DeleteBridgePort returning errors, but have `removeBridgePort` (links layer) treat cleanup failure as a logged warning rather than a removal failure — consistent with the pre-existing pattern where `ovs.Delete` logs cleanup errors and returns nil.

step-start
{
  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_1a89c860c001RGYFNYuY5bV7YV",
  "sessionID": "ses_e576ad8faffem1CYutQx182uFJ",
  "messageID": "msg_1a89c7c4c0018adhnQEFRoDsAF"
}
Reasoning
Confirmed. Errors from `removeBridgePort` propagate through `removeApplyLinkEndpoints` and `deleteApplyEndpoints`, aborting apply/reconcile; and through `DefaultNode.Delete` aborting container deletion during destroy. Base had no such failure source in the notfound path (nil) — in the found path, only LinkDel errors. So this is a new failure mode introduced by the PR when `ovs-vsctl` fails.

But how likely is ovs-vsctl to fail during destroy? If the user deleted the OVS bridge or ovs-vswitchd is down. Then: LinkByName on the ovs endpoint — for the container node's Delete, the container-side endpoint does LinkDel (succeeds), then removeBridgePort on... wait no. `Endpoint.Remove` is called per endpoint of the LINK. For a veth link between container A and ovs bridge B: A's node Delete iterates its endpoints → link.Remove → iterates both endpoints → A-side ep.Remove: LinkByName(A-side veth name) found → LinkDel → both veths die → then removeBridgePort(A's node = container A) → no deleter → nil. Then B-side ep.Remove: LinkByName(B-side name) → notfound → removeBridgePort(ovs node) → DeleteBridgePort → ovs-vsctl. If ovs is down → error → `Link.Remove` returns error → `DefaultNode.Delete(A)` returns error → destroy aborts for node A; the container A is NOT deleted. That's a genuine regression: previously the container was deleted successfully and only the port leaked.

That's a solid P3 (reliability). Trigger: ovs-vswitchd down/bridge deleted during destroy, or transient ovsdb error. Impact: destroy fails and container is left running. Correction: make `removeBridgePort` (or its OVS implementation) best-effort in removal paths — log and continue — consistent with `ovs.Delete`'s existing log-and-continue behavior.

Hmm — but wait: is it appropriate to keep errors for the apply path? The apply path `removeApplyLinkEndpoints` currently propagates; but for reconcile, failing the whole reconcile because a stale OVS port couldn't be cleaned is also questionable — but that path is designed to abort on real errors. The simplest correction: `removeBridgePort` logs the cleanup error and returns nil. Downside: reconcile loses signal — but the leak is what we were trying to fix; failing reconcile doesn't fix the leak either. Log-and-continue at the links layer is proportionate.

Now — one more check on the `Endpoint.Remove` found path: after `netlink.LinkDel(link)` for the ovs-side endpoint (ovs node in root ns; the port netdev found and deleted) → then removeBridgePort → DeleteBridgePort. For the valid-name case, removeEndpointPorts: DeletePort (port row might still exist) → deletes. Good.

Now let me make sure about the claim in Finding 1 regarding `AddLinkToContainer` — re-read head ovs.go lines 115-175 to confirm exact code.

300: func setOVSEndpointID(iface, endpoint string) error {
301: 	out, err := exec.Command(
302: 		"ovs-vsctl",
303: 		"set",
304: 		"Interface",
305: 		iface,
306: 		"external_ids:"+ovsEndpointExtID+"="+ovsMapValue(endpoint),
307: 	).CombinedOutput()

315: func ovsInterfaceNames(condition string) ([]string, error) {
316: 	out, err := exec.Command(
317: 		"ovs-vsctl",
318: 		"--format=csv",

338: func ovsInterfaceDeviceMissing(iface string) (bool, error) {
339: 	out, err := exec.Command(
340: 		"ovs-vsctl",
341: 		"--data=bare",
342: 		"get",
343: 		"Interface",
344: 		iface,
345: 		"error",
346: 	).CombinedOutput()

348: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("get OVS interface %q error: %w (%s)", iface, err, out)

350: 
351: 	return ovsDeviceMissing(string(out)), nil
352: }
353: 
354: // ovsDeviceMissing reports whether an OVS interface error column says the netdev is gone.
355: // A live interface returns "[]".
356: func ovsDeviceMissing(errorColumn string) bool {
357: 	errorColumn = strings.TrimSpace(errorColumn)
358: 
359: 	return strings.Contains(errorColumn, "could not open network device")
360: }
361: 
362: func ovsMapValue(value string) string {
363: 	if value == "" {
364: 		return `""`
365: 	}
366: 	for _, r := range value {
367: 		switch {
368: 		case r >= 'a' && r <= 'z', r >= 'A' && r <= 'Z', r >= '0' && r <= '9':
369: 		case strings.ContainsRune("-_.:@", r):
370: 		default:
371: 			return fmt.Sprintf("%q", value)
372: 		}
373: 	}
374: 
375: 	return value
376: }

Reasoning
All confirmed. Now let me finalize the findings and write the review.

Findings (final):

**P3 — OVS cleanup sweep runs per endpoint attach and per endpoint delete, scaling quadratically with ovs-vsctl process spawns**
- Location: head nodes/ovs/ovs.go:115-121 (DeleteBridgePort), 140-182 (AddLinkToContainer), 87-110 (Delete).
- Problem: `DeleteBridgePort` always runs `removeMissingAutoPorts()`, which lists ports (1 `ovs-vsctl` exec) and then runs one `ovs-vsctl get Interface <port> error` exec per `clab-*` port (ovs.go:262-281). `AddLinkToContainer` (ovs.go:169) now calls `DeleteBridgePort` on every link attach, and `ovs.Delete` calls it once per endpoint (ovs.go:89) before the trailing sweep. For a bridge with P long-named (invalid Linux name) endpoints, the missing-port scan costs 1 + P execs; per attach that's ~4 + P ovs-vsctl invocations where base code did a single `AddPort`. Over a full deploy of N endpoints the total becomes O(N²) process spawns (N=100 → on the order of 5,000+ `ovs-vsctl` forks), each a fork/exec plus an ovsdb round-trip. Also within a single `DeleteBridgePort` call, `removeEndpointPorts` (ovs.go:257) and `removeMissingAutoPorts` both call `ListPorts`, doubling that exec. This is inference from code, not a measurement.
- Impact: deploy and destroy of OVS-heavy topologies with >15-char interface names slow down quadratically.
- Suggestion: in `AddLinkToContainer`, call `n.removeEndpointPorts(logical)` instead of the full `DeleteBridgePort` — this version tags every port whose netdev name differs from the logical name, so the tag lookup already covers same-version stale ports; the missing-netdev sweep only matters for pre-upgrade leftovers, which `ovs.Delete`'s trailing sweep handles. In `ovs.Delete`, call `removeEndpointPorts` per endpoint and keep a single trailing sweep instead of one sweep per endpoint. Additionally reuse one `ListPorts` result per `DeleteBridgePort` call.

**P3 — OVS cleanup failures now abort destroy and apply/reconcile**
- Location: head links/ownership.go:165-205 (RemoveOwnedInterface, removeBridgePort), head links/endpoint.go:142-165 (Remove), propagation to nodes/default_node.go:472-478 (DefaultNode.Delete) and core/apply.go:318-332 (removeApplyLinkEndpoints).
- Problem: base code treated the interface-not-found case in `RemoveOwnedInterface` and `Endpoint.Remove` as a no-op; the found path could only fail on `netlink.LinkDel`. Now both paths call `removeBridgePort`, whose OVS implementation (`DeleteBridgePort`) can fail when `ovs-vsctl`/ovsdb is unavailable or the bridge was deleted. In destroy, `DefaultNode.Delete` returns the first endpoint error (default_node.go:474-477), so a failed OVS port cleanup aborts that node's deletion — the container is left running and the remaining nodes are not cleaned. Previously an OVS outage during destroy merely left silent port leaks. In apply/reconcile, `removeApplyLinkEndpoints` and `deleteApplyEndpoints` propagate the error and abort the whole operation.
- Trigger: destroy or reconcile a lab with an `ovs` bridge node while ovs-vswitchd is down or the bridge no longer exists.
- Suggestion: make the removal-path cleanup best-effort — have `removeBridgePort` (links layer) log the cleanup error and return `nil`, consistent with `ovs.Delete`'s existing log-and-continue behavior (ovs.go:87-110). Keep `DeleteBridgePort` itself erroring so `AddLinkToContainer` can still fail fast.

Hmm wait — if `removeBridgePort` swallows errors, then during destroy of the OVS node itself (`ovs.Delete`) the behavior is unchanged (already log-and-continue). And apply path: `RemoveOwnedInterface` notfound → cleanup error swallowed → fine. Good, consistent.

**P4 — per-port `ovs-vsctl get` failure in `removeMissingAutoPorts` treats a concurrently deleted port row as an error**
- Location: head nodes/ovs/ovs.go:266-269, 338-352.
- Problem: `removeMissingAutoPorts` lists ports and then runs `ovs-vsctl get Interface <port> error` per matching port. If a port row disappears between the list and the get (another `clab` process, or destroy racing deploy on a shared OVS bridge), the `get` fails and the error aborts the sweep; from `AddLinkToContainer` it fails the link deploy even though the port is already gone.
- Correction: treat a "no such row" failure as already-removed and continue.

**P4 — integration test fails instead of skipping when ovs-vsctl is installed but ovsdb is not running**
- Location: head nodes/ovs/ovs_test.go:41-63.
- Problem: the skip guard only checks root and `exec.LookPath("ovs-vsctl")`. A developer with the OVS packages installed but the ovsdb service not running passes the guard and then `AddBridge` fails, marking the unit suite failed. On GitHub runners (`make test`, cicd.yml:145-157) `ovs-vsctl` is absent, so the test skips there.
- Correction: also probe ovsdb connectivity (e.g., treat the initial `DeleteBridge`/`AddBridge` error as a skip reason), or attempt a cheap `ovs-vsctl show`.

Hmm, `_ = DeleteBridge(bridge)` ignores the first error; `AddBridge` fails. So probe with a lightweight command. Good.

No schema or user docs changes were made by the PR; the OVS kind documentation does not describe port cleanup, so nothing is stale, though a release note for the bugfix would be appropriate (not required).

- Not flagged: `removeBridgePort` deleting a non-owned port named exactly like the endpoint in the not-found path (no realistic trigger with coexisting same-name endpoints in the root netns); `ovsMapValue` uses Go quoting for special characters, adequate for the restricted name set; no release note added under `docs/rn/` (only governs edits there, none made).
